, as required.

∎

Lemma 9.43.

Let
e
be a WG expression, with

\emptyset;\emptyset\vdash e:T
, such that
e\mapsto d
, then
for all
d^{\prime}
such that
d\longrightarrow^{\ast}d^{\prime}

either
d^{\prime}
is a value,
d^{\prime}\longrightarrow d^{\prime\prime}
for some
d^{\prime\prime}
, or

d^{\prime}
panics on a
non-synthetic
type-assert.

Proof.

By induction on the compilation, we show that for any synthetic cast
of the form
d=E[n.(\lfloor{T}@{V}\rfloor)^{\circ}]

with
\Delta;\Gamma\vdash n:{T}@{U}
, the assertion

n.(\lfloor{T}@{V}\rfloor)^{\circ}
reduces (i.e. never
panics).
There are three places where such casts are added in the compilation.

•

\Delta;\,\Gamma\vdash e.f\mapsto e^{\prime}.i.{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({\lfloor S_{i}\rfloor}@{\lfloor S_{i}\rfloor})}
with

\overline{f\;{S}@{V}}=\mathit{fields}_{\Delta}(T)
, in the compilation
of field select (Rule
Select-boxed
).

•

e^{\prime}.m_{D}[\overline{{\lfloor S\rfloor}}](\overline{d^{\prime}}).{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({\lfloor T[\eta]\rfloor}@{\lfloor T[\eta]\rfloor})}

with

m[\overline{\beta~S^{\prime\prime}}](\overline{y\>\Phi})\>{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{T}@{V}}\mathbin{\in}\mathit{methods}_{\Delta}(R)

and

\eta=(\overline{\beta\mathbin{:=}S})
in the compilation of static and dynamic
method calls (Rules
Call
S
-boxed
and
Call
I
.

•

\neg\mathit{iface}(T^{\prime}_{i})\implies d_{i}=y_{i}.(\lfloor{T_{i}}@{T^{\prime}_{i}}\rfloor)
in adapter methods (to
unbox arguments that should not be boxed). Here we have that each

y_{i}
has type
{\lfloor\langle\Phi_{i}\rangle\rfloor}

with
\overline{\Phi}=\overline{{T}@{T^{\prime}}}
(see Figure
17
).

In each case the cast’s main-type is exactly the type
T
of
n
,
so by Lemmas
9.32

and
9.33
the value
n
evaluates to

{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}{T}@{U}}(S,\rho,w)
with
S\mathbin{\texttt{<}\mkern-1.0mu\raisebox{0.86108pt}{{:}}}_{\emptyset}T
; hence
Rule
assert-ok
S
(if
\neg\mathit{iface}(T)
) or Rule

assert-ok
I
(if
\mathit{iface}(T)
) applies.
Thus, by LWG reduction, all such casts always reduce successfully.
∎

Theorem 9.44 (
\bumpeq
is a Weak Bisimulation).

The relation

\bumpeq

is a weak bisimulation with respect to
LWG reduction well-typed terms.

Proof.

We show that the relation

\mathcal{B}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{def}}}{{=}}\{\,(d_{1},d_{2})\,\mid\,d_{1}\bumpeq d_{2}\,\}

is a weak bisimulation (with
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
well-typed). We proceed
by structural induction on the
\bumpeq
derivation.

Case 1: Reflexivity.

If
d\bumpeq d
, then trivially if
d\longrightarrow d^{\prime}
, we have
d\longrightarrow d^{\prime}
and
d^{\prime}\bumpeq d^{\prime}
.

Case 2: Symmetry.

If
d_{1}\bumpeq d_{2}
is derived from
d_{2}\bumpeq d_{1}
, then the bisimulation property follows by swapping the roles of
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
.

Case 3: Make operation equivalence.

If
e.({S}\rightarrow_{\rho}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}\Phi})\bumpeq{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}\Phi}(S,\rho,w)
with
e\bumpeq w
where
w
is a value:

•

Forward:
If
e.({S}\rightarrow_{\rho}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}\Phi})\longrightarrow d_{1}^{\prime}
, then by LWG reduction rules:

–

If
e
is already the value
w
, then
w.({S}\rightarrow_{\rho}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}\Phi})\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}\Phi}(S,\rho,w)
.
The right-hand side is already this value, so we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}\Phi}(S,\rho,w)\bumpeq{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}\Phi}(S,\rho,w)
.

–

If
e\longrightarrow e_{1}
, then
e.({S}\rightarrow_{\rho}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}\Phi})\longrightarrow e_{1}.({S}\rightarrow_{\rho}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}\Phi})
.
Since
e\bumpeq w
and
w
is a value, by induction hypothesis
e_{1}
gets closer to
w
.
Eventually
e\longrightarrow^{\ast}w
, so
e.({S}\rightarrow_{\rho}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}\Phi})\longrightarrow^{\ast}w.({S}\rightarrow_{\rho}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}\Phi})\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}\Phi}(S,\rho,w)
.

•

Backward:
Since
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}\Phi}(S,\rho,w)
is a value, it cannot reduce further.
This case is vacuous.

Case 4: Synthetic type assertion equivalence.

If
e\bumpeq e^{\prime}.({T}@{V})^{\circ}
with appropriate typing conditions:

•

Forward:
If
e\longrightarrow e_{1}
, we need to show
e^{\prime}.({T}@{V}^{\circ})\longrightarrow^{\ast}d_{2}^{\prime}
with
e_{1}\bumpeq d_{2}^{\prime}
.
By induction hypothesis,
if
e
diverges, so does
e^{\prime}
, so we focus on the case when

e
reduces to a value
w
.
Then we have to show that the type assertion succeeds which follows from Lemma
9.43
.

•

Backward:
By induction hypothesis,
e
and
e^{\prime}

either both correspondingly diverge, get stuck, or reduce to a
value. We focus on the case where they reduce to a value.
Pose
e\longrightarrow^{\ast}w
and
e^{\prime}\longrightarrow^{\ast}w^{\prime}
, with

w\bumpeq w^{\prime}
by induction hypothesis.
By Lemma
9.43
, we have

w.({T}@{V}^{\circ})\longrightarrow w^{\prime}
, and the result
follows since
w\bumpeq w^{\prime}
.

Case 5: Synthetic cast.

The cases for

{\Delta;\,\Gamma\vdash e\bumpeq e^{\prime}.({S}\rightarrow_{\rho}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}{T}@{V}})}
,

{\Delta;\,\Gamma\vdash e\bumpeq e^{\prime}.({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}T}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}T}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}@}{S})}
, and

{\Delta;\,\Gamma\vdash e\bumpeq e^{\prime}.({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}U}\twoheadrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}T})}
(static-change) are all
similar and straightforward since they only update the ghost type
information.

Case 7: Congruence.

We consider each specific syntactic construct:

Case 7a: Variables.
If
x\bumpeq x
, this is trivial since variables cannot reduce.

Case 7b: Dynamic method calls.
If
d.m[\overline{S}](\overline{f})\bumpeq d^{\prime}.m[\overline{S}](\overline{f^{\prime}})
with
d\bumpeq d^{\prime}
and
\overline{f\bumpeq f^{\prime}}
:

•

Forward:
If
d.m[\overline{S}](\overline{f})\longrightarrow d_{1}^{\prime}
:

–

If reduction is in receiver context:
d\longrightarrow d_{1}
, then by IH
d^{\prime}\longrightarrow^{\ast}d_{1}^{\prime\prime}
with
d_{1}\bumpeq d_{1}^{\prime\prime}
, so
d^{\prime}.m[\overline{S}](\overline{f^{\prime}})\longrightarrow^{\ast}d_{1}^{\prime\prime}.m[\overline{S}](\overline{f^{\prime}})
.

–

If reduction is in argument context:
f_{i}\longrightarrow f_{i}^{1}
, then by IH
f^{\prime}_{i}\longrightarrow^{\ast}f_{i}^{\prime 1}
with
f_{i}^{1}\bumpeq f_{i}^{\prime 1}
, so
d^{\prime}.m[\overline{S}](\overline{f^{\prime}})\longrightarrow^{\ast}d^{\prime}.m[\overline{S}](\overline{f^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,f_{i}^{\prime 1},\ldots})
.

–

If method call reduces to body substitution when receiver and arguments are values, the same reduction applies by equivalence.

•

Backward:
Symmetric argument.

Case 7c: Static method calls.
If
d{\#}T.m[\overline{S}](\overline{f})\bumpeq d^{\prime}{\#}T.m[\overline{S}](\overline{f^{\prime}})
with
d\bumpeq d^{\prime}
and
\overline{f\bumpeq f^{\prime}}
:
Similar to dynamic method calls but with static dispatch.

Case 7d: Field selection.
If
d.i\bumpeq d^{\prime}.i
with
d\bumpeq d^{\prime}
:

•

Forward:
If
d.i\longrightarrow d_{1}^{\prime}
:

–

If
d\longrightarrow d_{1}
, then by IH
d^{\prime}\longrightarrow^{\ast}d_{1}^{\prime\prime}
with
d_{1}\bumpeq d_{1}^{\prime\prime}
, so
d^{\prime}.i\longrightarrow^{\ast}d_{1}^{\prime\prime}.i
.

–

If
d
is a struct value
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0.5}T}\{\overline{v}\}
and
d.i\longrightarrow\Phi_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\downharpoonright_{1}}(v_{i})
, then since
d\bumpeq d^{\prime}
, we have
d^{\prime}
reduces to an equivalent struct, so the same field selection applies.

•

Backward:
Symmetric argument.

Case 7e: Type assertions.
If
d.(T)\bumpeq d^{\prime}.(T)
with
d\bumpeq d^{\prime}
:
Similar to field selection, but with type assertion reduction rules.

Case 7f: Dynamic operations.
If
\circ(\overline{d})\bumpeq\circ(\overline{d^{\prime}})
with
\overline{d\bumpeq d^{\prime}}
:

•

Forward:
If
\circ(\overline{d})\longrightarrow d_{1}^{\prime}
, either by reducing arguments in context or by applying the operation when all arguments are values. By IH, the same reduction sequence applies to
\circ(\overline{d^{\prime}})
.

•

Backward:
Symmetric argument.

Case 7g: Static operations.
If
\circ{\#}T(\overline{d})\bumpeq\circ{\#}T(\overline{d^{\prime}})
with
\overline{d\bumpeq d^{\prime}}
:
Similar to dynamic operations but with static type information.

Case 8: Structural equivalences.
For assignability-based
equivalences like
T(c)\bumpeq U(c)
when
T\prec:_{\Delta}U
,
all are values so the result is immediate.
∎
